Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Are the veteran materials player worn, or game worn?
goblue9815 October, 2018 at 14:49
Most likely Player worn if history is any indication. Panini seems to be moving completely away from Game Worn football cards, although their basketball products are mostly game used.
Ryan Cracknell17 October, 2018 at 12:30
@goblue – It varies from player to player and set to set. Best to do is check the back (or ask for scan) to know for certain.

8 COMMENTS
IamNotARobot19 October, 2018 at 11:07
I’d hate to ask again, but are the veteran and retired player swatches game worn or player worn? It makes all the difference in the world for me as a collector.
Ryan Cracknell19 October, 2018 at 11:43
@IamNotARobot – Again, it will likely depend on the player. Most products are a mix now. Once the cards come out, backs will say.
goblue987 December, 2018 at 14:16
Hi @IamNot,
I totally agree with you. As a collector, there is nothing worse then seeing Player Worn cards of guys I need. Case in point is Devin Funchess, why in the world is Panini putting out his Player Worn stuff in 2018 when he was a Rookie in 2015, that’s inexcusable. @Ryan is right though, it’s a mix but they often trend to Player Worn/Event Worn.
Buck City Breaks12 December, 2018 at 10:07
Football gets knocked because of the “player worn” stuff, but you have to realize that the 16 game season limits the amount of game worn jerseys that Panini has access to. With basketball and baseball, there is no shortage of games, which is why there is an abundance of game used gear, but it’s not like Panini gets first dibs on the very limited selection of NFL jerseys. A lot of these jerseys are traded between players, auctioned off for charity, or held as a keepsake for the player.
Yes, as collectors we all want game used gear, but it’s just not feasible in football like it is other sports
goblue9812 December, 2018 at 17:28
@BuckCityBreaks
I completely understand what you are saying, and I think that would be true for acquiring a lot of the star player jerseys, but for a guy like Funchess are you telling me that there is such a demand that Panini couldn’t acquire a few.
TAYLOR SIAS20 January, 2019 at 22:35
Can you get booklets in packs? I know that might be a silly question but I can’t tell what size they are.
Ryan Cracknell22 January, 2019 at 14:39
@Taylor – I believe the books are in hobby packs. If you check on the back of retail boxes and packs, it will list exactly what’s available.
Jeremy16 May, 2020 at 23:09
I found a retail box at Target yesterday, on clearance. The only decent card was a Nick Chubb Gold #’d 3/10.

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

8 COMMENTS
IamNotARobot19 October, 2018 at 11:07
I’d hate to ask again, but are the veteran and retired player swatches game worn or player worn? It makes all the difference in the world for me as a collector.
Ryan Cracknell19 October, 2018 at 11:43
@IamNotARobot – Again, it will likely depend on the player. Most products are a mix now. Once the cards come out, backs will say.
goblue987 December, 2018 at 14:16
Hi @IamNot,
I totally agree with you. As a collector, there is nothing worse then seeing Player Worn cards of guys I need. Case in point is Devin Funchess, why in the world is Panini putting out his Player Worn stuff in 2018 when he was a Rookie in 2015, that’s inexcusable. @Ryan is right though, it’s a mix but they often trend to Player Worn/Event Worn.
Buck City Breaks12 December, 2018 at 10:07
Football gets knocked because of the “player worn” stuff, but you have to realize that the 16 game season limits the amount of game worn jerseys that Panini has access to. With basketball and baseball, there is no shortage of games, which is why there is an abundance of game used gear, but it’s not like Panini gets first dibs on the very limited selection of NFL jerseys. A lot of these jerseys are traded between players, auctioned off for charity, or held as a keepsake for the player.
Yes, as collectors we all want game used gear, but it’s just not feasible in football like it is other sports
goblue9812 December, 2018 at 17:28
@BuckCityBreaks
I completely understand what you are saying, and I think that would be true for acquiring a lot of the star player jerseys, but for a guy like Funchess are you telling me that there is such a demand that Panini couldn’t acquire a few.
TAYLOR SIAS20 January, 2019 at 22:35
Can you get booklets in packs? I know that might be a silly question but I can’t tell what size they are.
Ryan Cracknell22 January, 2019 at 14:39
@Taylor – I believe the books are in hobby packs. If you check on the back of retail boxes and packs, it will list exactly what’s available.
Jeremy16 May, 2020 at 23:09
I found a retail box at Target yesterday, on clearance. The only decent card was a Nick Chubb Gold #’d 3/10.

8 COMMENTS
IamNotARobot19 October, 2018 at 11:07
I’d hate to ask again, but are the veteran and retired player swatches game worn or player worn? It makes all the difference in the world for me as a collector.
Ryan Cracknell19 October, 2018 at 11:43
@IamNotARobot – Again, it will likely depend on the player. Most products are a mix now. Once the cards come out, backs will say.
goblue987 December, 2018 at 14:16
Hi @IamNot,
I totally agree with you. As a collector, there is nothing worse then seeing Player Worn cards of guys I need. Case in point is Devin Funchess, why in the world is Panini putting out his Player Worn stuff in 2018 when he was a Rookie in 2015, that’s inexcusable. @Ryan is right though, it’s a mix but they often trend to Player Worn/Event Worn.
Buck City Breaks12 December, 2018 at 10:07
Football gets knocked because of the “player worn” stuff, but you have to realize that the 16 game season limits the amount of game worn jerseys that Panini has access to. With basketball and baseball, there is no shortage of games, which is why there is an abundance of game used gear, but it’s not like Panini gets first dibs on the very limited selection of NFL jerseys. A lot of these jerseys are traded between players, auctioned off for charity, or held as a keepsake for the player.
Yes, as collectors we all want game used gear, but it’s just not feasible in football like it is other sports
goblue9812 December, 2018 at 17:28
@BuckCityBreaks
I completely understand what you are saying, and I think that would be true for acquiring a lot of the star player jerseys, but for a guy like Funchess are you telling me that there is such a demand that Panini couldn’t acquire a few.
TAYLOR SIAS20 January, 2019 at 22:35
Can you get booklets in packs? I know that might be a silly question but I can’t tell what size they are.
Ryan Cracknell22 January, 2019 at 14:39
@Taylor – I believe the books are in hobby packs. If you check on the back of retail boxes and packs, it will list exactly what’s available.
Jeremy16 May, 2020 at 23:09
I found a retail box at Target yesterday, on clearance. The only decent card was a Nick Chubb Gold #’d 3/10.

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Tommy B19 May, 2018 at 07:09
How incredibly gaudy and flashy…
I love it. A great break from the standard purple, teal, orange, red, blue, green, gold and black parallels.
300 Base Cards at $90-$100 a box, I’m not sure this is for me…but I’m digging it more than the past two years.
Kerry19 May, 2018 at 09:21
I didn’t know the 90’s were back in town! Die-cuts, bright colors, crazy backgrounds…these are what drew me to stay in the hobby back in 1994.
IAmNotARobot14 August, 2018 at 12:57
It seems like Panini’s product lines are starting to blur…is this XR, Elite, Unparalleled? Too much stuff that looks the same. I know, if I don’t like it I don’t have to buy it. I miss the innovation that the competing companies had years ago before single companies had a monopoly on each sport.
Sean31 August, 2018 at 12:57
They looks so good.
The Bursts look amazing. Can’t stop staring at my 1 of 1 Baker RC. So shiny.

Tommy B19 May, 2018 at 07:09
How incredibly gaudy and flashy…
I love it. A great break from the standard purple, teal, orange, red, blue, green, gold and black parallels.
300 Base Cards at $90-$100 a box, I’m not sure this is for me…but I’m digging it more than the past two years.
Kerry19 May, 2018 at 09:21
I didn’t know the 90’s were back in town! Die-cuts, bright colors, crazy backgrounds…these are what drew me to stay in the hobby back in 1994.
IAmNotARobot14 August, 2018 at 12:57
It seems like Panini’s product lines are starting to blur…is this XR, Elite, Unparalleled? Too much stuff that looks the same. I know, if I don’t like it I don’t have to buy it. I miss the innovation that the competing companies had years ago before single companies had a monopoly on each sport.
Sean31 August, 2018 at 12:57

Ryan Cracknell

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).
